      U.S. Dips Into Strategic Oil Reserve Again Amid Supply Disruptions
      2h ago · ~8 min read
      The DOE authorized an emergency exchange of up to 400,000 SPR barrels to Phillips 66's Lake Charles refinery after a temporary crude delivery disruption, as oil prices climb above $100.
      Key points
      • DOE authorized up to 400,000 barrels from the West Hackberry SPR site to Phillips 66's Lake Charles refinery; the cause of the delivery disruption was not specified.
      • Lake Charles can process about 264,000 barrels per day, so the authorized volume is roughly a day and a half of its capacity.
      • The government calls it an exchange, not a sale: Phillips 66 must return the borrowed barrels plus additional crude as a premium.

      Nokia's comparable EPS of €0.09 beat the 8-cent consensus as revenue rose 10% to €5.15 billion on AI & Cloud; free-cash-flow guidance was cut.
      Key points
      • Comparable EPS from continuing operations was €0.09 (10 cents), up from €0.06, versus the Zacks Consensus Estimate of 8 cents.
      • Revenue rose 10% to €5.15 billion; AI & Cloud net sales surged 123% in constant currency, and IP Networks grew 43%.
      • Reported net profit was €156 million (€0.03 per share), up from €80 million, but higher restructuring and integration costs weighed on operating profit.
      • Free cash flow was negative €99 million; 2026 free cash flow conversion is now 35-55% of comparable operating profit, down from 55-75%.
      • The €2.1-€2.6 billion comparable operating profit outlook was maintained, and fourth-quarter net sales are expected to grow 20-24% sequentially.
      META's Q3 Earnings Beat Estimates, Revenues Rise Y/Y on Ad Growth
      1d ago · ~4 min read
      Meta's Q3 2026 revenue rose 26% to $64.6 billion on ad growth, but a $9.89 billion legal charge cut operating income 36.8%.
      Key points
      • Q3 adjusted EPS of $7.4 rose 2.1% year over year, beating the Zacks Consensus Estimate by 13.50%.
      • Revenue of $64.6 billion rose 26% year over year, with advertising at $63.06 billion, up 25.9%.
      • Total costs rose 68.1% to $51.61 billion, including a $9.89 billion charge tied to the August 2026 teen mental health litigation agreement.
      • Operating margin fell to 20% from 40%; excluding the legal charge, operating income would have risen 11%.
      • Q4 2026 revenue guidance is $73 billion to $76 billion, and capex guidance was narrowed to $135-$145 billion.
      IBM Sinks 7% as CEO’s AI Spending Caution Overshadows Earnings Beat; Accenture and Infosys Ease Back
      1d ago · ~4 min read
      IBM beat Q3 estimates but fell 7% after CEO Krishna warned of AI spending with 'no real return'; revenue forecast unchanged.
      Key points
      • Q3 2026 revenue was $17.1 billion versus a $16.84 billion estimate; adjusted EPS was $2.95 versus $2.87 consensus.
      • IBM stock fell 7% to $233.50, while Accenture dropped 0.5% to $239 and Infosys 2% to $12.44, suggesting company-specific damage.
      • Full-year constant-currency revenue growth forecast stayed at 4% to 5%, disappointing investors, though the free cash flow target was raised by more than $1 billion.
      • IBM Z mainframe revenue fell 20%, consulting grew just 0.2%, and total debt reached $63.8 billion.
      • Krishna urged clients to "pick a few" AI initiatives and scale them rather than run "100 little experiments."
      Meta Falls 3% as Higher AI Spending Outlook Overshadows Earnings Beat; Alphabet Dips, Microsoft Holds Steady
      1d ago · ~4 min read
      Meta beat Q3 revenue and guidance estimates but raised AI capex to $135B–$145B, sending shares down 3%; Alphabet fell 2%, Microsoft held steady.
      Key points
      • Q3 2026 revenue was $64.6B, up 26.1% year over year and above estimates; Q4 guidance of $73B–$76B also beat expectations.
      • Diluted EPS was $7.40 excluding a large legal settlement charge, beating estimates.
      • Capex guidance narrowed to $135B–$145B from $130B–$145B, and total expense guidance rose to $175B–$179B.
      • Q3 capex was $34.9B and free cash flow was negative $2.12B; operating margin fell to 20% from 40%.
      • Ad impressions rose 10% and average price per ad rose 14%; Zuckerberg says Muse is compute-intensive and must scale before turning profitable.
      Carpenter Technology (CRS) Q1 Earnings Beat Estimates
      1d ago · ~3 min read
      Carpenter Technology beat quarterly EPS estimates at $3.25 per share but missed revenue estimates, with $798.2 million in sales for the quarter ended September 2026.
      Key points
      • EPS of $3.25 beat the Zacks Consensus Estimate of $2.97, a surprise of +9.43%, up from $2.43 a year ago.
      • Revenue of $798.2 million missed the Zacks Consensus Estimate by 1.18%, though it rose from $733.7 million a year ago.
      • Carpenter has beaten EPS estimates four times over the last four quarters and topped revenue estimates two times.
      • Zacks Rank #2 (Buy) suggests the shares are expected to outperform the market; they are up about 16.9% year to date versus the S&P 500's 13.3%.
      • Consensus expects $3.05 EPS on $809.24 million revenue next quarter; peer Metallus reports November 2.
      Procter & Gamble earnings beat as sales rise 4%, boosted by laundry and home products
      1d ago · ~2 min read
      P&G beat earnings as volume and sales rose, led by fabric and home care, while healthcare fell; it raised its fiscal 2027 sales growth outlook mainly due to the Thorne acquisition.
      Key points
      • Volume growth of 2.4% marked a strong start to the new fiscal year; P&G reported just one quarter of volume growth in fiscal 2026.
      • Fabric and home care volume rose 4.3%, helped by laundry detergent and fabric enhancers; beauty and baby, feminine and family care each grew more than 2%.
      • Healthcare volume fell 2.9% on weaker oral care such as Crest and personal care such as Pepto-Bismol; grooming volume shrank 0.4%.
      • P&G reiterated fiscal 2027 core EPS guidance of $6.89 to $7.11 and raised all-in sales growth to 2% to 4% from 1% to 3%, reflecting the Thorne acquisition, which closed Oct. 1.
      • CFO Andre Schulten described a bifurcated economy and an intense competitive environment, with P&G balancing pricing against innovation.
      TSLA: Tesla Stock Falls 3% as Spending Cuts, Shrinking Profits Cloud Earnings Beat
      1d ago · ~2 min read
      Tesla beat adjusted earnings estimates (48 cents vs 45) on flat $28.05 billion revenue, but profits fell and shares slipped 3.2% after hours.
      Key points
      • Adjusted earnings were 48 cents per share versus 45 cents expected; revenue was broadly flat at $28.05 billion.
      • Reported net income fell 43% to $787 million and operating profit fell 56% to $718 million; operating margin was 2.6% versus 5.8%.
      • Automotive gross margin excluding regulatory credits improved to 18.1% from 15.4%.
      • Annual capital-expenditure forecast was cut to $20 billion from more than $25 billion, and free cash flow turned positive at $1.53 billion.
      • Cybercab public sales are targeted by year-end, pending federal safety review; commercial Optimus deliveries are targeted around the end of 2027, and a potential SpaceX merger is still under evaluation.

      SAP shares climb after Q3 earnings beat and strong cloud revenue outlook
      2d ago · ~1 min read
      SAP's Q3 non-IFRS EPS of €1.89 and revenue of €10.27 billion beat estimates; it raised its full-year revenue outlook, and shares rose over 4% after hours.
      Key points
      • Non-IFRS EPS €1.89 vs €1.83 expected; total revenue €10.27 billion vs €10.10 billion expected.
      • Cloud revenue rose 26% to €6.67 billion; current cloud backlog grew 28% to €24.14 billion.
      • Full-year cloud revenue now expected at the high end of the €25.8–26.2 billion range; operating profit guidance of €11.8–12.2 billion maintained.
      • Non-IFRS operating profit rose 18% to €3.03 billion; free cash flow up 36% to €1.72 billion.
      • About €4.2 billion of shares repurchased through September under a €10 billion multi-year buyback.

      Tesla reports Q3 earnings beat, positive free cash flow, and better-than-expected capex
      2d ago · ~4 min read
      Tesla beat on adjusted EPS ($0.48 vs. $0.44) and free cash flow ($1.53 billion), while revenue slightly missed; capex guidance cut to $20 billion.
      Key points
      • Revenue of $28.05 billion vs. Bloomberg consensus of $28.13 billion; adjusted EBITDA of $3.90 billion vs. $3.66 billion.
      • Free cash flow of $1.53 billion vs. an estimated -$4.92 billion; capex of $4.732 billion vs. an estimated $7.96 billion.
      • Full-year capex guidance cut to $20 billion from $25 billion; CFO says it will be funded primarily from operating cash flows.
      • Q3 deliveries of 486,532 were down 2% year over year but above the 463,000 Bloomberg consensus; energy storage hit 13.7 GWh.
      • NHTSA sent Tesla 21 questions on how it self-certified the Cybercab; answers are due Oct. 30, not the Nov. 20 Tesla requested.

      SpaceX stock jumps 10% after earnings beat and Musk hints at Tesla tie-up
      2d ago · ~2 min read
      SpaceX beat Q3 revenue estimates with $14.6 billion, up 161%, and guided Q1 2027 above consensus; Musk floated a Tesla merger.
      Key points
      • Q3 revenue of $14.6 billion, up 161% year over year, beat LSEG consensus of $12.72 billion.
      • Operating income was $2.8 billion and net income $4.4 billion, versus a net loss of $1.05 billion a year earlier.
      • Q1 2027 revenue is forecast at $33 billion to $37 billion, against an average analyst estimate of $22.2 billion.
      • The Super Intelligence segment's revenue rose ninefold to $7.56 billion from $813 million.
      • Musk said a Tesla combination has merit and both boards formed committees to discuss it; SpaceX shares rose 10% Wednesday.
      NextDC slashes earnings forecast, triggering 40pc share plunge
      3d ago · ~1 min read
      NextDC warned it will miss earnings forecasts, wiping $3 billion off its value; shares fell 41.7 per cent after Melbourne expansion delays.
      Key points
      • NextDC warned investors it would miss earnings forecasts and will consider selling assets, cutting spending and putting power cables underground at a major Melbourne west site.
      • Shares plunged 41.7 per cent to a nine-year low, wiping $3 billion from the company's value.
      • NextDC, Australia's third-biggest data centre owner by installed capacity, was forced to delay expansion at its new flagship Melbourne data centre.
